Written trace: Guided Practice selects scenario vocabulary (deduped, max 12 terms × 80 chars) and a grammar ID; `onReady` puts that context in route state; LiveRoute bounds it again and WorkerClient serializes it. Worker rejects invalid/oversized untrusted context, places the terms in a data-only prompt, resolves grammar IDs from D1, and emits a link/reference only when the AI marks a correction and echoes that resolved ID exactly. The client checks the nested response identity; the correction and mistake store it additively; SRS review retains it, shows the linked Arabic rule and German example only when IDs match, and review/progress sync validates and round-trips the optional reference. Without Guided Practice context, without a D1 grammar row, or for a correct/unmatched correction, the link is empty; previous stored records remain valid. T1 full Vitest passed `npm test -- --run`: 63 files / 730 tests; related browser regression group passed `timeout 540 npx playwright test e2e/journey.spec.ts --grep 'Guided Practice rehearses real lines and grades honestly|Live Interaction runs a turn with the orb and keeps the typed path open' --workers=1 --reporter=line`: 2/2. | V8-1. Fix the `journey.spec.ts:320` flake at its cause | done | commit `8313808`. **Root cause (proven, not guessed):** `e2e/harness.ts` `mockBackend`'s `bridge.speak()` held the scripted audio level for exactly **6 animation frames**; under load the app's rAF-driven analyser (`src/lib/audio/useMicLevel.ts` → `useVoiceCapture.ts` `decideStop`) can miss that window, so `spokeAtMs` stays `null`, `decideStop` returns `no_speech` after `NO_SPEECH_MS` (7 s), `/ai/transcribe` is never called, the utterance stays unconsumed and the composer stays empty → the 15 s assertion timeout. **Fix (no sleep):** the fake capture device now holds the level until a harness `AnalyserNode` (created in `ensureVoice()` after the gain node, `fftSize = 1024`, plus `context.resume()`) measures RMS > `AUDIBLE_RMS` (0.05) sustained for `SPEECH_HOLD_MS` (400 ms), bounded by `SPEECH_CEILING_MS` (1500 ms) so a broken probe cannot hang the suite; the constants cross into the page through the serialized `page.evaluate` argument (module scope is not visible in the page — a first attempt failed with `AUDIBLE_RMS is not defined`). `silence()` also waits for the recording button (`RECORDING_ACTION = /إيقاف التسجيل/`) to reach `count(0)` with a 10 s timeout. **Evidence:** `E2E_TARGET=preview npx playwright test` → **37/37 twice** (3.0 min each; `/tmp/v8-suite1.log`, `/tmp/v8-suite2.log`); the spec alone 3/3 unloaded (`/tmp/v8-rep2.log`, 26.9 s) and **8/8 with six CPU-saturating node loops** (`/tmp/v8-stress.log`, 69 s); per-test time ~8.6 s, i.e. far below the 14 s `no_speech` path, which is what proves speech is now actually detected rather than the assertion merely being generous. Gates: `npx tsc -p e2e --noEmit` clean; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm test` **67 files / 799 tests**. |
| V8-2. Real-D1 proof of the sync compare-and-swap and crypto exactly-once | done | local `wrangler dev --local` (port 8799, `--var ENVIRONMENT:test TEST_MODE:1`, throwaway local `NOWPAYMENTS_IPN_SECRET`/`ADMIN_SECRET`/`HMAC_SECRET`) on a **wiped `.wrangler/state`**; probe run from gitignored `.wrangler/v8-probe/` (`probe.mjs` + a wrapper `entry.js` that re-exports only `default`) and **deleted afterwards**; nothing pointed at production, no remote binding, no production D1 write, no real secret. **Result: 23/23 assertions passed** (`.wrangler/v8-probe/results.json`, `run.log`, `dev.log`). **Sync CAS:** first sync `{success:true,rev:1}` → second `rev:2` → a stale `base_rev:1` → **409 `{error:"sync_conflict",code:"SYNC_CONFLICT",rev:2}` with the `rev` unchanged in D1** → two **simultaneous** `base_rev:2` writers → **exactly one 200 `rev:3`, the other 409** (no double commit) → the loser follows the shipped client protocol (`workerClient.ts` 409 → `restoreProgress` → one retry with the fresh rev) → 200 `rev:4` → `/progress/get` and the raw row both hold the union (`session_summaries` `sA`+`sB`, `mistakes` `mA`+`mB`, `saved_word_ids` `[1,2,3]`, `sync_revisions.rev = 6` at the end) — **no write lost**, now on a real engine, not `FakeD1`. Legacy (no `base_rev`) merge never decreases counters. **Crypto exactly-once:** unsigned → 401 `missing_signature`; valid sig over a tampered body → 401 `signature_mismatch`; first `finished` IPN → `{delivered:true}`, code readable via `GET /crypto/order` with the claim token; the identical `payment_id:status` replayed → **`{duplicate:true,delivered:false}` with the code byte-identical** (D1 `crypto_events` PRIMARY KEY, `event_key` count = 1 per row); **two simultaneous identical IPNs → exactly one delivered**; `price_amount` 4.00 against a 5.00 order → `{reason:"price_mismatch"}`, order `failed`, `code` NULL; a `confirming` status → `classification:"pending"`, not delivered. **Two findings recorded, neither fixed this run** (see UNPROVEN): V8-F0 (sync 500s on a D1 that has never run `/verify`) and V8-F1 (`wrangler dev` cannot boot the real entry — non-function named exports). |

| V9-1. `handleProgressSync` creates its own tables (V8-F0 fixed) | done | commit `4d4e40e`. **Cause (proven in V8-2, fixed here):** `handleProgressSync` was the one ledger writer that never called `ensureLedgerTables`, so the first `POST /progress/sync` against a D1 that had never redeemed a code threw `D1_ERROR: no such table: sync_revisions` on its conditional `UPDATE`, and the catch-all answered a bare `{error:"server_error"}` 500 — a 5xx that could not even be recorded, because `error_reports` did not exist either. **Fix:** `ensureLedgerTablesOnce(env)` — the ledger bootstrap memoized per D1 binding in a `WeakMap` (a module-level boolean made a *second*, still-empty database look ready; failed attempts are deliberately not memoized so the next request retries) — is awaited before the sync path's first statement, and the degrade condition is now `if (!env.DB || !env.USER_PROGRESS || !syncTablesReady)`: a database that cannot be prepared falls back to the KV mirror with **200** instead of 500ing. The same ensure now guards `/auth/session`'s registry write, `/client-error`'s report, `checkGlobalRateLimit`'s counters and the admin failure recorder. `handleDeleteUser` also deletes the authoritative `sync_revisions` row and treats the legacy `user_progress` delete as best-effort (that table exists nowhere; its DELETE failed every deletion with 500 `DELETE_INCOMPLETE` and skipped the registry purge). **Regression test:** new `tests/freshDatabase.test.ts`, 5 tests, with a strict `FreshD1` double that models a real empty SQLite database — it throws `D1_ERROR: no such table: <t>` for a statement against a table no `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` created, and throws on any statement shape it does not model, instead of inventing the table the way `tests/accountOps.test.ts`'s local double and `tests/helpers/fakeD1.ts` do, which is exactly why a green suite could not see this. Proven to fail **4/5 without the fix** (worker file reverted, suite re-run) and pass 5/5 with it. |
| V9-2. Every ledger-table writer ensured — and the admin failure throttle actually fires | done | commit `4d4e40e`. Mission item 2 ("grep every handler that touches `sync_revisions` or ledger tables without ensuring them; list each") produced this inventory. **Ensured explicitly:** `handleAuthSession` (`:343`, new) · `checkGlobalRateLimit` (`:545`, new) · `consumeTrialQuota` (`:625`) · `handleDeleteUser` (`:791`) · `recordError` (`:1143`, new) · `handleVerify` (`:2066`) · the referral payout (`:2313`) · `handleProgressSync` (`:2437`, new — V9-1) · `recordAdminAuthFailure` (`:2903`, new) · `handleAdminRoutes` (`cloudflare-admin.js:871`/`:909`, now the injected full batch). **Not ensured, degrades by design (each documented in place):** `isAdminThrottled` (`:2933`) reads the counter in a try/catch → `{throttled:false}` (the recorder creates the table on the first failure, so request 2+ is throttled) · `handleProgressGet` (`:2589`) reads `sync_revisions` in a try/catch → KV fallback, `rev 0` · `sweepExpiredRows` (`:1318-1319`) wraps each delete → `-1` and returns no driver text · `cloudflare-admin.js` `getRegistryStats`/`getUserRedemptionCount` catch per query → `0` (the `users` aggregate falls back to `empty`) · `withVerifyRegistry`/`withAiTelemetry` call `recordActivity` (a plain INSERT) but the whole hook body is in a try/catch and the wrapped handler has already ensured. **The one genuinely broken site was the admin gate:** `cloudflare-admin.js:786` carries a deliberate second copy of the bearer-secret check (a back-import would be a cycle — `cloudflare-crypto.js` imports *from* `cloudflare-admin.js`), and that copy **never called `recordAdminAuthFailure`**. Because the worker's `:1489` throttle only *checks* the counter before delegating, `/admin/api/*` answered 401 for ever with no counter row written, so the per-IP lockout the comment at `:1487` promises ("cannot get past 5 failures per 15 minutes") could never engage on the dashboard's own data endpoints — the exact brute-force surface it claims to protect; and it recorded nothing even when the tables did exist. Found by the V9 probe (N3 `[401×6]`, N3b `0` rows). **Fix (no new import):** the worker injects `recordAdminAuthFailure` and `ensureLedgerTablesOnce` through `handleAdminRoutes(..., deps)`; the admin gate calls the recorder on a failed compare, and the admin surface creates the *full* ledger batch (it reads `redeemed_codes_ledger` for its redemption counters, which `ensureRegistryTables` never created). The duplicated compare also now normalizes lengths before the constant-time loop, byte-for-byte mirroring the worker's gate — the raw compare bailed early on a length mismatch and leaked the secret's length. **Tests:** 3 new tests in `tests/adminContent.test.ts` pin the injected-recorder contract (called once on a length-mismatched wrong secret and still 401; not called on success; the injected full-ledger ensure is used); the recorder test fails without the fix. |

The two accessibility defects V40 measured but did not fix | done | Owner line present: `DEPLOY-AUTHORIZED: merge, pages` — a bare "deploy" was refused first, because §3 requires the exact line and `docs/agent/DEPLOY.md` precondition 1 says STOP without it. **Found by re-measuring the built bundle, not by re-reading the code.** (1) **The action slot announced a control that was not on screen.** The orb and the send plane cross-fade in one 56px slot, and the half not showing was hidden with `opacity-0 pointer-events-none` only. Measured with an empty field: `disabled=true`, `tabIndex=0`, and still in the accessibility tree — so a screen reader walked from the sentence field into an invisible "أرسل جملتك" and then into the microphone; with text typed it was the reverse, the microphone staying announced behind the send button. **Fix:** `aria-hidden` for the a11y tree, `disabled` + `tabIndex={-1}` for the tab order, and the existing `pointer-events-none` for hit-testing, applied to whichever half is not showing. **`inert` was tried first and rejected on evidence:** a type declaration makes the build green, but React 18.3 never writes the attribute — measured on the built bundle, `inertAttr=false` with no warning anywhere — so a "fixed" build would have hidden nothing; a unit test now pins that the source does not reach for it. The orb also states its disabled state in the tab order (`tabIndex={disabled ? -1 : 0}`), because a disabled <button> still accepts a programmatic `focus()` in Chromium. (2) **The viewport meta blocked pinch-zoom** (`maximum-scale=1.0, user-scalable=no`, WCAG 1.4.4) — the item V9-10 filed as "unfixed" and that no gate could see, because axe rates `meta-viewport` *moderate* while the B6 gate filters to critical/serious. **Fix:** the meta now allows zoom and adds `viewport-fit=cover`, which is what makes the dock's `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` real on a notched phone; the behaviour the old tag was bought for — a double-tap zooming the page mid-drill — is kept where it is wanted, with `touch-action: manipulation` on controls, `pan-x pan-y pinch-zoom` on the transcript, and `overscroll-behavior: none` so a pinch at the end of the chat cannot bounce the page. axe on the live screen now reports **zero** violations at any impact; the `color-contrast` failure V9-10 filed on `/` and `/welcome` **no longer reproduces** (axe: 0 violations at any impact, same bundle) — it was measured against the V9-era `index-B3aF3j7l.js` and superseded by the V37/V38 palette work. **Regressions:** new `e2e/conversationA11y.spec.ts` (4 tests — the hidden half is absent from the a11y tree and unreachable by Tab *and* by script, in both directions; the cross-fade still sends and the field still clears; zero WCAG A/AA violations at any impact with and without text; the round counter never claims a round past the last) and new `tests/v40ConversationA11y.test.ts` (10 source contracts). Both new e2e tests were proven to fail on the pre-fix bundle — `aria-hidden` reverted, rebuilt, 2 failed at exactly the `toHaveCount(0)` assertions. Nothing failed locally because this machine runs **Node 24.14.0**. **Fix, without skipping, loosening or deleting a single check:** declared the requirement where a human and CI can both see it — `package.json` `engines.node` = `^22.22.2 \|\| ^24.15.0 \|\| >=26.0.0`, the intersection of the dependency floors (jsdom 30.1.1; vitest 5.0.1 `^22.12.0 \|\| ^24.0.0 \|\| >=26.0.0`; lighthouse 13.5.0 `>=22.19`; wrangler 4.136.3 `>=22`) — and pinned **both** CI jobs to `"24.15.0"`, the lowest version that satisfies it, quoted as a full version so the guard can compare it exactly. CI run **`36568057274`** on `616e273` — **the first fully green run in 16 pushes**: `verify` **success**, `e2e` **success**, `secret-scan` **success**, and CI's own numbers match local exactly (`node-version: 24.15.0`; `Test Files 74 passed (74)`; `Tests 891 passed (891)`; `PASSED — 3 file(s) checked (2 module(s), 1 supplement(s))`; `dist/assets/index-Dz12j0HD.js 497.26 kB`; `37 passed (3.9m)`). **First-green-since evidence:** the same steps that had been failing since `9fc3d38` passed unchanged. **Both pushed commits verified, not just the first:** run `36568057274` on the code commit `616e273` and run `36569510240` on the docs commit `1c3b885` — `verify`, `e2e` and `secret-scan` **success** in both, and the Pages git build produced production deployments `01ba79c3-1ccb-4eb6-be64-3bf8aac2f001` and `3bead164-be4d-446a-b043-c41bc704336b` from them. |
| V17-2. The guard: a test that fails on the old pins and keeps the gates in place (STEP 1) | done | New `tests/ciWorkflow.test.ts`, **12 tests**, and the floor it enforces is *causal, not magic*: it scans `scripts/*.mjs` for direct `.ts` imports (today `audit-curriculum.mjs` and `load-curriculum.mjs`) and requires `engines.node`'s lowest allowed version to be ≥ **22.18.0** (`TYPE_STRIPPING_FLOOR`), the release where `import './x.ts'` stopped needing a flag. It then requires every `node-version:` in **every** file under `.github/workflows/` to be a **full `x.y.z`** that satisfies the range — a bare major is rejected deliberately, because `24` resolves to the latest release of the line and the comparison could not be trusted. It asserts the *running* Node can load the repo's own code, so a developer on Node 20 gets a named failure instead of the worker crash. And it keeps the gates honest: jobs `verify`, `e2e` (with `needs: verify`) and `secret-scan` must all still exist; `verify` must still run all ten commands (including both content audits, `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` and `npx tsc -p e2e --noEmit`); and the workflow may contain no `continue-on-error`, no `\|\| true` and no `if: false`. **Proven both ways against the real file, not only fixtures:** with the historical pins put back in place the guard fails — `line 37: node-version "20" must be a full x.y.z — a bare major resolves to the latest release of the line and cannot be checked against engines.node "^22.22.2 \|\| ^24.15.0 \|\| >=26.0.0" (floor 22.22.2)`, and the same for line 75 — and with the fix in place it passes 12/12; `ci.yml` was restored byte-identically each time (`sha1 1ca1fb846e096b5e2bcd8ffe3e720df46de7a97f` compared before and after). **Deliberate, recorded limit:** the local-machine assertion is a **capability** check (type stripping), not `satisfiesRange`, because this machine runs **24.14.0** — one patch below jsdom's declared floor — and the suite is green on it; the declaration is about what CI and users must have. The honest consequence, not hidden: `npm ci` prints one `EBADENGINE` warning about this repo's own `engines.node` locally until the owner upgrades (OWNER-OPEN). |

| V26-FIX-RETYPE. Session-report retype drill rejected a correct full sentence | done | Found in the V26 signed-in walkthrough (`docs/agent/AUDIT-V26-product-experience.md` context): the debrief shows the AI's corrected **fragment** and grades with `gradeAnswer`, which is exact-match (`normalize` + article-only `close`), so a learner who writes the whole sentence — `Ich habe den Bericht jetzt fertiggestellt` for the fragment `ist jetzt fertiggestellt` — was marked wrong. **Fix:** new pure `gradeCorrectionRetype(expected, actual)` in `src/lib/srs/engine.ts` — same verdicts as `gradeAnswer` plus: after setting aside function words (articles, pronouns, auxiliaries of haben/sein/werden), the correction counts as reproduced when its content words appear as a contiguous run inside the learner's answer **or** vice versa (learner typed only the shown fragment). Deterministic, no model. `SessionReportScreen.handleValidateRetype` now calls it; `gradeAnswer` is unchanged so vocab/review recall stays exact. Deliberate documented trade-off: a negated answer that still contains the fragment passes — safer for a low-stakes retype than rejecting a correct sentence. **Regression tests:** 7 added to `tests/srsEngine.test.ts`, including the exact walkthrough pair and a test pinning that `gradeAnswer` still returns `wrong` for it (proves the bug and the split). **Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npx vitest run tests/srsEngine.test.ts` 28/28; `npm test` **89 files / 1062 tests** (was 89 / 1055 — +7). No deploy, no D1, nothing pushed. **Related, NOT fixed (reported):** `PracticeScreen.handleValidateMistakeRetype` has the same exact-match false negative and additionally sets `isMastered: true` from a single retype, contradicting `MASTERED_REPS = 3`; recorded in APP-MAP §10 and MEMORY §E. |

Read-only — reads three documents and asks git two questions; never writes, deploys or touches a secret. Pure parsers (`extractNext`, `section`, `tableRows`, `manifestCounts`, `lastVerified`, `plainText`, `clip`, `wrap`) are exported and pinned by `tests/sessionStart.test.ts` (12 tests) including a marker-that-it-is-short assertion (<70 lines) and a synthetic-ledger test that the active NEXT is chosen over an archived one. Wired into `AGENTS.md` §1 (run it first) and documented in APP-MAP §8/§9. **Verification:** script runs and prints the real readout; `npx vitest run tests/sessionStart.test.ts` 12/12; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npm test` **90 files / 1074 tests** (was 89 / 1062 — +1 file, +12 tests). |

| V26-FIX-PRACTICE-RETYPE. PracticeScreen retype: shared grader + store-owned mastery | done | Closes the two defects the session-report fix left behind in `PracticeScreen`. **(1) Matcher:** the handler compared the retyped answer to the correction with an ad-hoc exact match (`input.replace(/\.$/,'') === target.replace(/\.$/,'')`), so a correct full sentence around the corrected fragment was rejected — now `gradeCorrectionRetype(m.corrected, answer)`, the same grader the debrief uses. **(2) Mastery:** a single correct retype wrote `db.mistakes.update(id, { isMastered: true })`, contradicting `MASTERED_REPS = 3`; the drill now looks up the review item (`[kind+refId]` = `['mistake','mistake:<id>']`), calls `gradeReviewItem(item, 'good')` (or idempotent `enrolMistake` for pre-queue rows), and lets the store write `isMastered` on the third good recall. The mistake row is a live query, so the card flips reactively. | V28-1A. Stage 1A evidence: review + chat defect classification | done | Read-only, from the owner's signed-in browser profile (production origin) plus code tracing. **Local dump:** 4 mistakes / 5 review items (3 vocab `vocab:82-84`, 2 mistake) — too small to be the owner's real corpus, so the classes come from the code paths that create items. **Review defect classes (proven in code):** (1) *no clear question* — `newReviewItemFromMistake` set `promptAr = mistake.grammarRule || 'صيغة صحيحة'`, so the learner saw a rule label (sometimes German, e.g. `Perfekt mit haben und Partizip`) or a placeholder, never a question; (2) *answer not derivable* — the expected German was the AI's rewrite of the learner's own sentence, which was not shown; (3) *false-positive corrections* — capture wrote a mistake whenever `is_correct === false`, with no check that the correction changed anything meaningful (case/punctuation/transliteration included); (4) *one direction only* — every item was Arabic→German, never German→Arabic; (5) *bare fragments* — no cloze/context was guaranteed. **Chat defect classes (proven in code):** obstacle rotation by `turnIndex: userTurnsCount` with no "not on the first turn / not twice / not after a repeat request" gate; hints fall back to `sortOrder[0]` starter phrases with no validation that they answer the last AI message; correction shape is validated but not the "correct an already-correct sentence / no explanation" cases. **Honest limitation:** the 10-transcript rate was not measured from 10 live sessions (transcripts are not stored server-side); the classification is from code + harness fixtures, and the measured pass rates are recorded by the Stage 1C harness. |
| V28-1B. No deploy yet (Stage 1E). |

| V28-1C. Chat quality: validate the AI's own output (owner feedback #2) | done | New pure worker module `cloudflare-turn-quality.js`, wired into `/ai/turn`: **hints** — the embedded `next_hint` is kept only if `hintMatchesQuestion(reply_de, hint)` (a yes/no question needs a polarity answer or a topic echo; a slot wh-question needs the topic or the slot fill — time/place/number; an open question accepts any statement that is not another question), so the observed «Fehlt Ihr Gepäck? → Hier ist mein Pass» is dropped and the client falls back to the validated `/ai/hints` floor; **corrections** — `evaluateCorrection` rejects an incomplete correction, one that changes nothing once case/punctuation/umlaut-transliteration are folded, and one that "corrects" a sentence the learner already wrote; a rejected correction is returned as a clean turn (the grade and the sentence disagreeing is the honest outcome); **obstacles** — `obstacleInstructionForTurn` withholds the persona obstacle on turn 0 and whenever the learner just asked for a repetition, then rotates as before. **No extra AI call** — all three validators are pure and run on the single fused turn response. **Harness:** `scripts/eval-chat-quality.mjs` + `tests/fixtures/chatTurns.json` (30 turns incl. the observed bad ones) run offline, wired into CI's verify job; measured **hints 58%→100%, corrections 58%→100%, obstacles 50%→100%**. **Tests:** new `tests/turnQuality.test.ts` (9) incl. the before/after rates and a non-vacuity block; `tests/aiRoutes.test.ts` updated to encode the turn-0 gating. **Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `node --check` clean on every `cloudflare-*.js`; `node scripts/eval-chat-quality.mjs` OK; `npm test` **93 files / 1115 tests** (was 92 / 1106 — +1 file, +9). Not deployed yet (Stage 1E). **Deferred into 1D:** the deterministic conversation beats and the level-based turn cap. |

| V28-1F-AFTER. The deferred AFTER turn-latency measurement (live, signed-in production profile) | done | Measured on the **live** deployment (`0ab4658d` worker, Pages `058eeacb-db6c-4ae7-8545-56b5f5c7c4bc`) in a **separate tab of the owner's signed-in profile** so the owner's own tab was never touched. Method is identical to the V28-1F BEFORE samples: drove real turns through the UI and read `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')` for `/ai/turn`. **AFTER samples (n=6):** 4774 / 3140 / 2799 / 3458 (cafe_order, A1 practice, 4 turns) and 3416 / 5532 (doctor_visit, 2 turns) ms → **median 3437 ms**, mean ≈ 3543 ms. **BEFORE samples (n=3, prior run):** 20229 / 1483 / 1429 ms → median 1483 ms, mean ≈ 7714 ms. **Honest reading: this is NOT a controlled A/B.** The two sets were taken at different times against a third-party model provider (Gemini) with n=3 and n=6, and the BEFORE set is bimodal (a 20 s cold call plus a tight ~1.45 s pair) while the AFTER set is uniform (2.8–5.5 s). The change (deterministic beats, +~107 prompt tokens/turn) is not a plausible cause of a 2 s shift; provider latency is. **Conclusion carried as UNPROVEN-equivalent:** the AFTER median is recorded, but the comparison cannot attribute any difference to the code — the one firm statement is that the change did not obviously make turns faster, and a decision-grade before/after would need same-session interleaving of both deployments, which production cannot do. **Side observation during the same run:** 6 live turns produced **0** delivered `next_hint`s and **1** real correction (word order: `Ich habe Kopfschmerzen seit gestern` → `Ich habe seit gestern Kopfschmerzen`), a meaningful change, with **0** false-positive corrections across the 5 correct turns — a live spot-check, not a rate (n=6). Driving the turns wrote local practice sessions in the owner's profile (local Dexie only); no D1 write, no secret handled. |

| V28-5. Audit: daily-task streak + anti-farming XP against timezone changes, clock shifts and multi-device use | done | **FIXED — a completed review day could be un-completed by later work.** The review task is the only one whose rule reads LIVE state (`reviewDue`): emptying a short queue recorded the day complete, but a new item becoming due later the same day flipped the status back to not-done, which also made `completedToday` false and **dropped today from the streak** — so doing more work visibly reduced the streak. Fix: the stored `daily_tasks` row is now authoritative — new `DailyTaskEvidence.reviewCompleted` (set from `todayRow.review`) makes the review status monotonic, and `readDailyTasks.completedToday` is derived from the row flags instead of the live statuses, so the panel and the streak agree by construction. New regression test in `tests/dailyTaskStore.test.ts` (add a due item after the batch was recorded) **proven to fail on the pre-fix store** (`done` → false) and pass after; new pure case in `tests/dailyTasks.test.ts`. `npm test` **102 files / 1217 tests** (was 1215), `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. **VERIFIED NOT EXPLOITABLE — XP from an unmeasured session.** `sessionXp` pays an independence bonus at `accuracyPercent: null`, but `finishSession` is reachable only on the final turn (`useLiveConversation.ts:731-735`), so a session always has ≥1 learner sentence; the reachable unmeasured award is the 25-XP all-hint conversation, bounded by the 600/day cap. The frozen `sessionXp` rule is therefore unchanged — measurement over assumption. **VERIFIED NOT EXPLOITABLE — multi-device.** `stats` merges **Last-Write-Wins** (`mergeLatest` on `updated_at`, `workerClient.ts:80-86`), so two devices' `totalXp` never sum; and synced `session_summaries` are `bulkPut` into local `db.sessions` (`workerClient.ts:1136`), so after a sync `xpEarnedToday` sees the other device's sessions and the cap self-corrects — two devices cannot reach 2×600 in one day. **ACCEPTED LIMIT (documented, not faked) — clock/timezone.** The cap and the streak are keyed on the device-local day (`localDateKey`), so moving the clock forward (or crossing a timezone) starts a fresh local day and grants a fresh 600 XP and a new streak day. Inherent to a local-first design with no server authority for XP (§3 keeps the server authoritative for entitlements/quota/payments, not XP); a real fix is server-side accounting, which is out of scope. **BOUNDED — travel.** `dailyTaskStreak` ignores future-dated keys and collapses duplicate keys, so travelling west can add at most one day per crossing and eastward skips are absorbed by the one-day forgiveness. |

| V29-4. Daily XP + streak made server-authoritative (owner: "make daily XP and the streak server-authoritative so a device clock or timezone change can no longer grant a fresh day") | done (no deploy, no commit) | The daily cap and the streak were computed entirely on the device (`src/lib/progress/dailyXp.ts` + `src/lib/utils/streak.ts`), so moving the clock/timezone granted a fresh day, a fresh cap and another streak step. **New `cloudflare-daily.js`** owns the day: `serverDayKey(nowMs, offsetMinutes)` derives it from the SERVER clock shifted by the learner's offset, and the offset is **locked at first sync** (`clampOffset` −720..840) so a later device timezone can never move the boundary. On first creation the ledger is seeded from the client's `totalXp` + completed-day history (bounded — only real, non-future, non-absurd day keys) so shipping does not reset an existing learner; after that client history is ignored and only server-observed events advance state. `applyDailyEvents` is **idempotent by event id** (a retried offline batch credits exactly once), caps `xpToday` at `DAILY_XP_CAP = 600`, marks the three daily tasks and records an earned day; `sessionXpFor`/`dailyStreak` are the exact twins of the client rules (pinned equal by `tests/dailyAuthority.test.ts`). Storage is one KV record `daily:<sub>` (no D1 write, no new dependency). **Worker wiring:** `/progress/sync` processes the ledger ONCE before the D1 merge loop (so a conflict retry can never double-credit) and overlays `total_points`/`streak_days` in both the D1 and the KV-degrade path; `/progress/get` overlays too. **Ledger creation is not optional (hardened in V29-5):** every `/progress/sync` creates the ledger, a missing `daily` payload included, so a stats-only client cannot opt out of authority. A client's total is accepted only UPWARD and only as fast as `DAILY_XP_CAP × elapsed days`, so no single request can jump the lifetime total; the streak and the day stay server-owned. The one-time migration seed is bounded (`SEED_MAX_TOTAL = 1_000_000`, `SEED_MAX_STREAK = 400`). **Client:** new `src/lib/progress/dailyAuthority.ts` — a durable localStorage event queue (`katzu_daily_events_v1`, max 120) fed from `useLiveConversation` after `markScenarioTaskDone`, `buildDailyPayload` (offset `-getTimezoneOffset()`, the local total, completed-day history, today's row, the queued session events) and `adoptDailyAuthority`, which writes the server's `totalXp`/`streakDays`/`dailyAuthority` into the user row; `workerClient.ts` (`syncProgress`/`flushPendingSync`/`restoreProgress`) adopts the server answer and clears only the events the server confirmed; `readDailyTasks` prefers the server streak when present. **Gates:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `npx tsc -p e2e --noEmit` clean; `npm test` **105 files / 1266 tests** (was 1230; +16 across the new `tests/dailyAuthority.test.ts` and `tests/dailyRoute.test.ts`, +19 in the new adversarial `tests/dailyLedgerAdversarial.test.ts`, +1 for the sign-out queue wipe); `E2E_TARGET=preview npx playwright test` **62 passed**; `npm run build` OK; `node --check cloudflare-*.js` clean; the four content audits exit 0. **Adversarial probe (19 cases, `tests/dailyLedgerAdversarial.test.ts`) + the hardening follow-up (V29-5) that fixed what it landed.** The four landing attacks are closed: (1) a stats-only sync that omits `daily` no longer escapes authority — the ledger is created, the streak/day stay server-owned, and the lifetime total can rise only by `600 × elapsed days`; (2) the one-time seed is clamped, so a hostile first sync can no longer mint 1e9 XP or a years-long streak; (3) the KV lost-update is gone in-isolate via a per-account serialization lock, so two concurrent devices no longer drop each other's events; (4) the dedupe window is 2000 ids, so eviction is no longer cheap. Still BLOCKED: replaying an id, the clock/timezone change (offset locked), invalid/future seed days, unknown kinds, short ids, out-of-range offsets, and lifetime XP beyond one capped day per server day. Residual (recorded in MEMORY): two requests to *different* Cloudflare isolates can still race the KV read-modify-write (a loss, never inflation) — the only real fix is a D1/DO compare-and-swap. **Data lifecycle (V29-5):** `handleDeleteUser` now deletes `daily:<sub>` (it previously survived account deletion) and `/user/export` now includes it; `wipeUserScopedData` clears the localStorage daily-event queue so a signed-out learner's sessions cannot be credited to the next account on the device. **Committed + CI green (V29-5 follow-up):** the V29-4/V29-5 code landed in `e0ecd81` on `launch-hardening`; CI run **`37109392007`** is green (verify · e2e 62 passed · secret-scan). **DEPLOYED (V29-6):** the worker now carries `cloudflare-daily.js` (version `a510e25c-ed78-470e-92a6-810d38794cc1`) and production serves `assets/index-CfZ6VaJV.js`; see the V29-6 row. |
| V29-5. Adversarial findings fixed + public-launch-readiness pass (owner: "fix them, then make sure our app is ready for final public launch in all ways") | done (no deploy, no commit) | **Ledger hardening (all four landed attacks closed):** `/progress/sync` always creates the ledger (a missing `daily` payload is treated as empty) so a client cannot opt out; the lifetime total is reconciled only UPWARD and bounded to `DAILY_XP_CAP × elapsed days`; the first-sync seed is clamped (`SEED_MAX_TOTAL`/`SEED_MAX_STREAK`); a per-account async lock serializes the KV read-modify-write in-isolate (removing the concurrent lost-update); the dedupe window rose 400→2000. The D1 path now checks `base_rev` in a preflight so a request that will 409 never mutates the ledger. **Two real V29-4 data-lifecycle bugs found and fixed:** (a) `handleDeleteUser` did not delete `daily:<sub>`, so an account's server-authoritative ledger survived deletion — now a `daily_ledger` step that the deletion test asserts, and `/user/export` includes it for portability; (b) `wipeUserScopedData` did not clear the localStorage daily-event queue, so a signed-out learner's queued sessions would be credited to the next account on the device — fixed via a leaf `src/lib/progress/dailyQueueKey.ts` (avoids the `dailyAuthority ↔ katzuDb` import cycle) with a regression test. **Launch-readiness audit (public-launch lens):** `npm run build` OK · `npx tsc --noEmit` + `npx tsc -p e2e --noEmit` clean · `npm test` **105 files / 1266 tests** · `E2E_TARGET=preview npx playwright test` **62 passed** against the production bundle (run on port 3100 via a throwaway config because another thread held 3000) · `node --check cloudflare-*.js` clean · the four content audits exit 0 · `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` **0 vulnerabilities** · the CI `secret-scan` patterns locally clean. No source TODO/FIXME. **Verdict: code-ready; not launchable by an agent** — a **worker deploy** (required for V29-4/V29-3 to bite) and a **Pages deploy** both need `DEPLOY-AUTHORIZED`, and the public launch is gated by owner-only items unchanged from `LAUNCH-STATUS.md` §7/§8 (rotate `ADMIN_SECRET`; attach a domain so `robots.txt`/`sitemap.xml` resolve; payments provider + secrets; counsel-reviewed privacy/terms; real-device voice). **Committed + CI green:** `e0ecd81` (the V29-4/5 code) + `3f392aa` (the CI e2e analytics-config fix), CI run **`37109392007`** green (verify · e2e 62 passed · secret-scan). **DEPLOYED in V29-6** (`DEPLOY-AUTHORIZED: merge, worker, pages`): merged ff to `main` `559aa81`, worker `a510e25c-ed78-470e-92a6-810d38794cc1`, production bundle `assets/index-CfZ6VaJV.js`; see the V29-6 row. |

Palette, motion scale, accessibility sweep, tap targets and the `/ai/hints` cost ceiling (owner: "fix and improve all, then when all is good deploy everything online… think of what is best for the end user") | done (no deploy — no `DEPLOY-AUTHORIZED` line in the prompt) | **Palette (the reported problem).** Five values sat in the rose band at OKLCH h 339–344, ~50° off the lavender the app is built on: `kz-magenta`, `kz-magenta-deep`, `tertiary`, `tertiary.container`, `article.die`. Every one was a *valid* token, which is exactly why no check caught it — validity is not belonging. Now `174 123 255` (h 299) + deep `115 67 222` (h 291), `tertiary` → periwinkle `143 161 228` / `77 95 156` (h 272), `article.die` → `212 196 246` (h 300), and both glass tiers carry the brand hue (`16 13 26` canvas, `7 5 14` well). Measured on production: the complete set of chromatic hues painted on `/demo`, `/welcome` and `/signin` is **283, 288, 293, 294, 295, 298** — one arc, `roseCount: 0`. **Method note (LESSONS):** the first recommendation `#C77DFF` was rejected *because it was re-measured* — h 309 is still rose. **Motion (all 7 plans in `plans/` executed).** The declared ladder in `src/index.css` had **zero readers** — every transition in Katzu was silently using Tailwind's undeclared 150ms. `transitionDuration` (`fast`/`DEFAULT`/`panels`) + `transitionTimingFunction` (`spring`/`out`) now read those variables, and `--kz-dur-slow: 560ms` is retired as `--kz-dur-panels: 300ms` (nothing in the ladder exceeds the 300ms UI budget). Confetti is gated on `useReducedMotion` — it was the **only** animated subsystem ignoring the preference. `BottomSheet`/`Modal` gained a real entrance (`data-mounted` + rAF; their `transition-opacity` had been dead code because `if (!isOpen) return null` gives a transition nothing to interpolate from). The `GlassSurface` scroll handler derives position from `scrollY` inside a rAF instead of calling `getBoundingClientRect()` per scroll event on the screen learners scroll most. Six progress bars moved from inline `width` to `scaleX` with an RTL origin, and `AudioWaveform` from `height` to `scaleY`. All 61 remaining `transition-all` sites now name the property they animate (3 enumerate explicitly; `Button.tsx` untouched — already correct since V20). **Accessibility.** 19 form controls gained an `aria-label` (a placeholder vanishes the moment there is text, so the field was unnamed); 20 bare `outline-none` removed — a *utilities-layer* rule that was deleting the global focus ring, measured `outline: 2px solid rgba(0,0,0,0)`; verified live at `rgb(180,160,255) solid 1.9px` on keyboard focus; 4 icon-only buttons named (the `/study` bookmark also got `aria-pressed`); 3 sub-44px targets raised. **Small screens (measured, not assumed).** 16 routes at **320px** and at **200% zoom**: **zero** horizontal overflow and **zero** sub-44px targets after the fix — the worst pre-fix offenders were the two header sign-in buttons at **16px** tall, i.e. the only route into sign-in for a returning visitor. **Cost.** `/ai/hints` was `quotaExempt` (correctly — a learner mid-conversation must never lose the hint sheet) but had **no ceiling of its own**, leaving only the global 200/day: repeatedly asking for fresh hints was an uncapped bill from a free account, while `/ai/ask` had a tight 8/day. Hints now has its own `hints` scope (free 30 / Pro 120 per day, 6/min) and answers the limit as **200 with an empty `hints` array** rather than an error, because the client already falls back to the free starter phrases — a usable sheet beats a message. **New gates (hues and rules the old audits could not see).** `design-audit.mjs` gained three zero-budget rules: `unnamed-field`, `transition-all`, `focus-ring-suppression`. `tests/designSystem.test.ts` gained a `motion scale` block (each ladder variable is read by the config, declared once, and ≤300ms; no hardcoded `duration-NNN` anywhere in `src/`) and a `one hue family` block (every brand token stays out of the rose band; earned stays within 15° of the accent; the canvas-side colour copies in `borderBeamCss`/`KatzuOrb`/`KatzuPresence` and `tailwind.config.js` equal their tokens). **Gates:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean · `npx tsc -p e2e --noEmit` clean · `npm test` **106 files / 1323 tests** (was 1319; +4 hints-cost, +13 motion/hue/focus rules) · `node --check` clean on every `cloudflare-*.js` and `scripts/*.mjs` · `node scripts/design-audit.mjs` **Within budget** (new rules all 0) · `node scripts/contrast-check.mjs` **All contrast pairs pass** (tightest 4.52:1) · `npm run build` OK · `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` **0 vulnerabilities** · the four content audits exit 0 · `E2E_TARGET=preview npx playwright test` **62 passed** against the production bundle with SWs blocked. **Not done, deliberately:** the worker was NOT deployed (the hints ceiling is committed but **not live** — it needs `DEPLOY-AUTHORIZED: worker`), no secret or D1 write, no owner-only item touched. Carried UNPROVEN: the new entrances and the composite bars were verified by unit + e2e + computed style, not on a real phone; real-device voice remains the largest unverified surface. Owner items unchanged (rotate `ADMIN_SECRET`; `katzu.app`; imprint/refund wording; payments `test_mode`; the pasted `AQ.Ab8RN6…HbyMQ` value is not a Google client ID and should be rotated too). |

| V32-1. Make it stupid-simple to use in a glance (owner: "make the product simpel stupid to use, in a glance, especially for new people in german learning … especially chat and test and quizzes"; audit `docs/agent/EXPERIENCE-AUDIT-V32-SIMPLE.md`) | done (no deploy at write time — the deploy is the one authorized `merge, worker, pages` pass covering V31+V32) | **Measured before touching anything** (throwaway harness, 25 screens at 360px, since deleted): `/app/practice` **110 controls**, `/app/library` 16 with **12 competing primaries**, `/scenario/:id/study` 12 with **7 primaries of which 6 read identically «استمع إلى النطق»**, `/scenario/:id/practice` 30 with **two inputs sharing one placeholder**, `/app/profile` 29, `/app/write` 26, and `/demo` **2** — the demo is the standard the rest of the app failed. **P0-1 a quiz with no way out.** «السؤال التالي» was `disabled={!isAnswerSubmitted}`, so a learner who did not know had to guess to continue. `QuizScreen.tsx` now offers «لا أعرف — أرني الإجابة» (`handleReveal()` + `didReveal`) whenever the answer is not submitted, states «الصحيحة: X — لم تختر، فهذه لا تُحسب صحيحة» and enrols the item for review exactly like a wrong answer — the owner's decision, matching what Review already did. **P0-2 the practice hub was flat.** `PracticeScreen.tsx` gained `browseWords`: the default view is the six skill cards plus a «بنك الكلمات» card (`arCount(vocabulary.length, WORD_FORMS)`) and «تصفّح الكلمات»; the 48 rows exist only in the search view; the filter label «الكل» became «ابحث في الكل» (it was a filter, not a section heading). **P1-3** `TrailScreen` `TRAIL_PREVIEW_COUNT = 5` + «اعرض بقية المشاهد». **P1-4** every Guided Practice input got a visible `<label htmlFor>` (`grammar-answer` / `retrieval-answer`) and its own placeholder. **P1-5** every Study listen button is named by its phrase (`aria-label={\`استمع إلى نطق: ${p.german}\`}`). **P1-6** the Welcome name is **optional** (`DEFAULT_DISPLAY_NAME = 'متعلم'`; the button is no longer disabled) — the owner's decision. **P1-7** Ask has a real `<h1>` and a tappable «جرّب مثالاً» chip driven by the new `ASK_EXAMPLE_AR`. **P1-8** «بدء» → «ابدأ التدريب». **P1-9** chat preselects **تدريب** with a «موصى به» badge and a direct «ابدأ «تدريب» الآن», REAL one tap below — the owner's decision. **P1-11 / P2-11** the level picker renders only in `mode === 'signup'`. **P2-10** the quiz score line uses `arCount(score, CORRECT_FORMS)`. **P2-17 the standing gate.** New `scripts/simplicity-check.mjs` walks 26 screens with two rules that mean what they say — `control-budget` (per-file budgets) and `unnamed-heading` (accepts `<h1|h2|h3>`, which is why it reports 12 healthy screens correctly) — wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` next to the design gates and pinned by `tests/simplicityGate.test.ts` (5) plus `e2e/simplicity.spec.ts` (5). **A third rule was written, measured and then deleted:** `primary-budget` flagged **12 healthy screens**, because a source-level count of `GlassButton`/`variant="primary"` cannot distinguish a real competing action from a button inside a card. A gate that cries wolf is worse than no gate, and `tests/simplicityGate.test.ts` now **asserts its absence** so it cannot creep back. **Gates:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean · `npx tsc -p e2e --noEmit` clean · `npm test` **109 files / 1363 tests** · `npm run build` OK · `node --check` clean · design-audit **Within budget** · contrast **All contrast pairs pass** · simplicity-check **Within budget** · `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` **0 vulnerabilities** · the four content audits exit 0 · `tests/appMap.test.ts` 9/9. **Five e2e assertions had to move with the copy** (`accessibility`/`journey` 'بدء' → 'ابدأ التدريب'; `firstRun` `/^الكل \(/` → `/^ابحث في الكل \(/`; `dailyTasks` '1 يوم متتالٍ…' → 'يوم متتالٍ…' — no digit before the one, which is the V31 plural rule, not a weakened test). **One e2e flake found and fixed at the cause:** the first 67-test run failed `journey.spec.ts` 'the learner sees their own German' with "live-caption not found" and passed in isolation. Cause: the scripted recogniser held the interim result for a fixed 1500 ms, so on a loaded runner React batched the interim and the final into a single render and the caption never existed — the same family as the 2026-09-29 mic note (a fake that moves faster than the state it stands in for). `e2e/harness.ts` now holds the interim for **at least** the requested `holdMs` and then until `[data-testid="live-caption"]` is in the DOM, with one grace period as the ceiling; proven by `--repeat-each=3` (3 passed). Two intermediate attempts failed first and are recorded in LESSONS: a bare harness constant inside the injected body is **undefined in the page** (constants must travel through the args object), and finalising as soon as the caption renders finalises before `say()` returns. **A second e2e defect, in the new `simplicity.spec.ts`:** it timed out on the last question, waiting 30 s for an escape button that had correctly disappeared — the loop asked "is the result screen up?" right after clicking «عرض النتيجة», so the answer was always *no*, and the arbitrary `question < 12` cap was hiding that the deck is longer than that. Fixed by waiting on **either** outcome after each advance (`expect(options.or(finished))`) and removing the cap in favour of the result assertion. **A false alarm worth recording, because it looked like 17 product failures:** one full-suite run reported 17 failures with 26 asset **404s**, all in screens untouched by V32. Cause: `npm run build` was run *while* the e2e preview server was serving `dist/`, so hashed filenames changed under a live server. Re-run clean with nothing else touching the build: **67 passed**. Lesson: never run a build during a Playwright `preview` run — the 404s are the tell. **Backlog, explicitly not done:** P2-12 (numbered Listen/Write steps), P2-13 (collapsible profile sections), P2-14 (inline lock reasons), P2-15 (move the preferences nag), P2-16 (the chat "how to build it" affordance) — each restructures a screen rather than clarifying one. Carried UNPROVEN: the visual pass by eye (the preview webview does not composite, so the design verdict rests on computed styles + the two design gates) and real-device voice. Owner items unchanged.

| V34-1. Apple-polish and performance pass — one fluid-glass system, no mouse-only motion (owner: `/apple-design` — "improve the design and performance … focus on polish rather than redesign … unnecessary motions like the glare when mouse hovering which makes the app slow, also meaningless since our app is for phones") | done (no deploy at write time — the deploy is the one authorized `merge, pages` pass covering this row) | **Measured first, on the production bundle, with a committed tool.** `e2e/perf/perfprobe.spec.ts` (run via `e2e/perf/playwright.perf.config.ts`, excluded from the suite by `testIgnore`) reads Chromium's own `RecalcStyleCount` / `RecalcStyleDuration` / `TaskDuration` on Journey Home. **Before:** 5 idle seconds cost **46 style recalculations / 100.4 ms**; **200 pointer moves cost 200 recalculations / 969.1 ms** of style work and 48.3 s of wall clock; 12 scroll steps cost 27 recalcs / 62.3 ms. **After, same machine and same bundle path: 0 / 0 / 0 ms on all three.** Three causes, all removed rather than throttled. **(1) The pointer glare.** `useSpecularHighlight` followed the mouse and the scroll position, writing `--kz-spec-x/-y` on the Journey Home root — which repainted the `radial-gradient` under *every* glass panel on the screen, on every move. The hook, `SpecularHighlight` and the per-surface `.kz-specular` span are **deleted**; the material is now lit from a fixed direction (`--kz-spec-x: 28% / -18%`, declared once, written by nothing). A phone has no pointer to follow, so the whole cost bought nothing. **(2) Hover on a touch app.** Every `hover:` in `src/` (134 utilities across 34 files) became **`pointer-hover:`**, a Tailwind variant compiled under `@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` — a tapped card no longer stays looking selected, and a blurred surface is not repainted for a state a phone can never be in. The scrollbar thumb is gated the same way. **(3) An always-on animation driver.** The ambient `BorderBeam` ran one shared 30 fps rAF loop writing **17 custom properties per mounted beam, forever**, to breathe nine radial gradients; it was most of the idle cost on the one screen that carries it. The ambient role is now **painted, not driven** — the same three layers at their resting geometry plus a single 600 ms fade-in, `buildPulseConfig`/`attachPulse` and the whole rAF driver deleted, the IntersectionObserver now only watches the role that still moves. The travelling comet on an engaged control stays: it is meaning-bearing and pure CSS, so it never reaches the main thread. **Consistency and Apple standards.** (a) **One radius ladder:** the values moved into `src/index.css` as `--kz-radius-{tag,chip,control,panel,sheet,hero}` and Tailwind *reads* them (`radius('control')` → `var(--kz-radius-control)`), so `.kz-primary`'s private **22 px** and the secondary button's `control` **20 px** can no longer disagree — that 2 px was visible on two buttons side by side. (b) The `:focus-visible` rule's `border-radius: 4px` is **gone**: an outline already follows the element's own radius, so the line was overriding the ring into a square on every rounded control. (c) `prefers-reduced-transparency: reduce` and `prefers-contrast: more` now both have real answers — frostier/near-solid panels with the grain dropped, versus near-solid panels with a defined `--kz-glass-edge`, both keeping every colour and contrast ratio. (d) Tracking is size-specific on the Latin scale only (+0.015 em caption, −0.015/−0.01 em display/title) and **never positive on Arabic**, which is cursive and would come apart. (e) One product defect found while measuring: `scripts/smoke-token-hygiene.cjs` went **10/11** against the live site because V33 had folded **sign-out** inside «بياناتك» — the only control a learner reaches for when leaving needed an extra tap to find. Sign-out is hoisted below the fold-out sections and always visible (Apple puts Sign Out at the foot of the account list), and the third section is retitled «الحساب والخصوصية» to match what it now holds. **New standing gates:** `scripts/design-audit.mjs` gains `hover-on-touch` and `pointer-follow` (both budget 0; the two legitimate press-tracking sites carry a reviewed `design-audit: allow`, as does a shader uniform that happens to be named `hover`), `tests/v34ApplePolish.test.ts` (24) pins the four structural facts, `tests/designSystem.test.ts` now asserts the radius ladder is *read* rather than copied, `e2e/applePolish.spec.ts` (2) proves in a browser that nothing on Journey Home changes as the pointer crosses it and that the beam carries no driven custom properties, and `e2e/polish.spec.ts`'s ≤ 12 rendered-control bound still holds with sign-out visible again. **One existing assertion moved with the copy:** the V33 test that pins the Profile section titles now pins «الحساب والخصوصية» — the requirement it states (a folded group must say what it contains) is unchanged, only the string moved. A green `npm test` from *before* the rename was not evidence about the rename, so the suite was re-run afterwards and caught exactly this pin — which is why the gate is re-run after copy moves rather than carried over. **Gates:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx tsc -p e2e --noEmit` clean · `npm test` **111 files / 1404 tests** · `npx playwright test` **73 passed** against the production bundle (the probe excluded) · `npm run build` OK · `node --check` clean on every `cloudflare-*.js` · design-audit **Within budget** (10 categories) · simplicity-check **Within budget** · contrast **All contrast pairs pass** (tightest 4.52:1) · `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` **0 vulnerabilities** · the four content audits exit 0. **Measurement honesty, stated plainly:** these are Chromium main-thread counters under software rendering (SwiftShader), so they measure style/layout/script work, **not** GPU compositing or real-device frame rate; and the 48.3 s → 3.3 s sweep figure is dominated by Playwright's per-move round trip, so it means "the page stopped blocking input acknowledgements", not a user-perceived 14× speedup. Carried UNPROVEN: the visual pass by eye (the preview webview does not composite, so the design verdict rests on computed styles, the two design gates and the screenshot-free measurements above), real-device voice, and GPU behaviour on a real phone. Owner items unchanged.

Cross-discipline interface review (`/better-interface`) + the polish it asked for | done (no deploy at write time — rides the same one authorized `merge, pages` pass as `V34-1`) | **Scope:** the app's whole learner-facing surface at **320px and 412px**, in a real Chromium against the production bundle — 13 screens (`/`, `/welcome`, `/demo`, and all nine `/app/*` tabs) plus Profile, in their populated states, plus the keyboard focus walk. Written by the `better-*` domain skills, all six of which were available and applied in order; `better-colors` owns contrast and found nothing (see below). **The most valuable result of the review was three findings that turned out not to exist**, and each was caught by measuring rather than believing the first number: (1) "the Ask textarea has no accessible name" — my checker computed names without `<label for>`; recomputing the way the platform does (aria-label → aria-labelledby → `labels[]` → text) across all 13 screens gives **zero** unnamed controls, and `e2e/accessibility.spec.ts`'s axe run agrees. (2) "Journey Home scrolls sideways by 3–5px" — `scrollWidth` counts content that `body { overflow-x: hidden }` already clips; an actual sideways drag and `scrollTo(200,0)` both leave `scrollX` at **0** at 320px and 412px, so there is no user-visible overflow. (3) "five library card titles are truncated" — `scrollHeight` exceeded `clientHeight` by 3px, which is Cairo's Arabic ascenders/descenders overflowing a 16px line box, not clipping. **What was real, and is fixed:** the landing page's three footer links measured **55x20, 38x20 and 60x20** — under WCAG 2.5.8's 24px floor, let alone the project's 44px thumb target — and the Listening quick-replay pair «تشغيل بطيء 0.8x» / «إعادة» measured **83x17 and 25x17**, with the full-width «إنهاء التدريب والعودة» at 32px. All six now carry `min-h-touch` with `inline-flex items-center`; **measured after: landing 3 → 0 sub-44px controls, listening 8 → 5.** Two headings truncated their own words — the Journey Home chapter title and «اسأل كَاتْزُو عن الألمانية» — with nothing to reveal the rest; both dropped `truncate` for `text-balance` and now wrap. Three text fields rendered at **12–14px**, which is under iOS Safari's 16px focus-zoom threshold, so focusing one magnifies the whole page: the Ask textarea (14), the Writing textarea (14) and the Practice search field (12) are now `text-base`, and Practice's two filter chips (30px) got the 44px treatment with them. **Pinned in the browser, not in source:** three new tests in `e2e/polish.spec.ts` measure rendered hit areas and rendered font sizes, because a source-level assertion about a class name is exactly what this session proved unable to see. **Deliberately not changed:** the word-bank chips on Write and Listen (30px). They clear WCAG's 24px floor, they are 24–30 controls on a phone screen, and raising them to 44px would cost the layout more than it buys — the project's density decision stands, recorded here rather than silently. **`better-colors`: no finding.** `scripts/contrast-check.mjs` measures every token pair against the app background and a well, all pass (tightest **4.52:1**), and the V34 palette stays one violet axis. **Gates:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean · `npx tsc -p e2e --noEmit` clean · `npm test` **111 files / 1404 tests** · `npx playwright test` **76 passed** against the production bundle · `npm run build` OK · `node --check` clean on every `cloudflare-*.js` · design-audit Within budget · simplicity-check Within budget · contrast All contrast pairs pass · `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` **0** · the three content audits exit 0. **An honest correction to the V34 record:** `npx tsc -p e2e --noEmit` has been failing since `089cb54` — the new `e2e/perf/perfprobe.spec.ts` cast Playwright's closed `Metric` type to a `Record<string, number>` without going through `unknown`. It was reported clean because the command was run as `npx tsc -p e2e --noEmit | head`, so `$?` was `head`'s status. Fixed at the cast and re-verified unpiped; every gate quoted in this session is now reported with its own exit status. **Still UNPROVEN, unchanged:** the visual pass by eye (the preview webview does not composite), real-device voice, and `scripts/smoke-token-hygiene.cjs`, which reads **10/11 against production** because production still serves V33 — the sign-out fix it proves cannot land until this deploys, and that smoke must read 11/11 afterwards. Product-experience audit (`/product-experience-audit`) — walk every screen as a real user, panel the findings, auto-execute the approved plan | done (no deploy at write time — rides the same one authorized `merge, pages` pass as `V34-1`) | **The walk (real Chromium, 360x640, the production bundle, 27 `*Screen.tsx` files).** First run is honest and short: `/welcome` states one promise and offers 5 actions, `/signin` is a heading plus a Google button, `/demo` is a heading plus one action. **The core loop is 4 taps** from Trail to Study (Trail -> story -> practice -> study), 5 to the live conversation. Empty and locked states are already good — `/app/review` says «لا توجد مراجعة مستحقة الآن» with a way back, and every locked grammar lesson says «مقفل — يُفتح بعد إتمام …» by name. **Three findings, all real, all in the same family: a screen that cannot be named, or a failure that cannot be heard.** (1) **Guided Practice had no heading at all** — the only heading in the file was an `<h3>` for a grammar title at line 304, so a screen reader could never name the screen; it has 3 fields and no name. (2) **The Quiz screen had no heading either** — its only `<h3>` («أحسنت! أتممت الاختبار») renders on the *result* screen, so during every one of the questions the screen was unnamed. (3) **The Ask failure was silent** — with the backend answering 500 the card draws «تعذّر الحصول على شرح» and a «أعد المحاولة» button, but with no live region a screen reader is told nothing happened at all. **The gate that should have caught (1) and (2) was the reason it did not: `scripts/simplicity-check.mjs` `hasHeading()` accepted `<h3>`, so a deep section label counted as a screen name. Tightened to `<h1|h2>` — and measured before the fix, not after: running the strict rule over all 27 screens names **exactly two** files, both real, so the tightening costs two fixes rather than inventing a wall of defects. Its own guard test now evaluates the shipped expression (extracted from the gate source and run through `new Function`) against an h3-only screen and an h2 screen, so the rule cannot be quietly loosened again.** **Also fixed, from the same walk:** four surfaces that drew a result with no live region — Placement's answer feedback, Quiz's answer explanation, and the redemption screen's error and success banners — now carry `role="status"` / `role="alert"` as Listening and Writing already did. And **`document.title` never changed**, so 26 screens shared one tab title; it now follows the route (`<screen name> · كَاتْزُو`) via `titleForPath` in `App.tsx`, which also names `/app/coach` «ملف أخطائك» — the screen's own name — instead of the brand. **Pinned:** `tests/screenNames.test.ts` (5) asserts every declared route is named and that **no two routes collide** (the defect was four tabs all reading Katzu, so distinctness is asserted, not assumed) and that `/scenario/:id` does not swallow its own sub-routes; `e2e/screenNames.spec.ts` (4) proves it in a browser — the two formerly unnamed screens expose a non-empty `h1/h2` *in the state the learner is actually in*, the 500 failure is an `alert` that carries its retry, and `document.title` changes on a real navigation. `tests/simplicityGate.test.ts` keeps its twelve-false-reports guard and gains the h3 rejection.
